- [ ] **Step 8: Replace `closed.rs` wholesale**

Replace the whole of `src-tauri/src/closed.rs` with:

```rust
//! Stack of recently closed tabs, for Cmd+Shift+T.
//!
//! Only tabs showing a **saved file** are pushed — ⌘W on an untitled tab is a
//! deliberate, permanent discard (spec §8) — and nothing is pushed while
//! quitting: those windows come back through session restore instead.
//!
//! An entry remembers the window it was closed from, and ⌘⇧T puts the tab
//! back there while that window lives: a window has a project, and a tab
//! dropped into another project's window would break that binding. When the
//! window is gone the tab comes back in a new window, and the other tabs
//! closed with the same window follow it there (`revived`) instead of each
//! getting a window of its own.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::sync::Mutex;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

use crate::session::SessionState;
use crate::tabs::{RegTab, WindowTabs};

const MAX_ENTRIES: usize = 20;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct ClosedEntry {
    pub path: String,
    pub cursor: usize,
    pub top_line: usize,
    /// The window the tab was closed from.
    pub label: String,
    pub number: Option<u32>,
    /// That window's geometry in logical pixels, as `SessionState` records it
    /// — used when the tab has to come back in a new window.
    pub x: i32,
    pub y: i32,
    pub width: u32,
    pub height: u32,
}

/// Whether a closing tab should be pushed onto the stack at all.
pub fn should_push_on_close(is_quitting: bool, path: Option<&str>) -> bool {
    !is_quitting && path.is_some()
}

fn geometry_of(session: &SessionState, label: &str) -> (i32, i32, u32, u32) {
    session
        .snapshot_for(label)
        .map(|s| (s.x, s.y, s.width, s.height))
        .unwrap_or((0, 0, 0, 0))
}

/// ⌘W on one tab. The caret comes from the frontend at the moment of the
/// close; the heartbeat's copy is up to 5 s old.
pub fn record_tab_close(
    session: &SessionState,
    stack: &ClosedStack,
    label: &str,
    number: Option<u32>,
    path: &str,
    cursor: usize,
    top_line: usize,
) -> bool {
    if !should_push_on_close(session.is_quitting(), Some(path)) {
        return false;
    }
    let (x, y, width, height) = geometry_of(session, label);
    stack.push_entry(ClosedEntry {
        path: path.to_string(),
        cursor,
        top_line,
        label: label.to_string(),
        number,
        x,
        y,
        width,
        height,
    });
    true
}

/// A closing window's file tabs in push order: the active one last, so it is
/// the first one ⌘⇧T brings back.
pub fn close_order(window: &WindowTabs) -> Vec<&RegTab> {
    let (active, rest): (Vec<&RegTab>, Vec<&RegTab>) = window
        .tabs
        .iter()
        .filter(|t| t.path.is_some())
        .partition(|t| window.active.as_deref() == Some(t.id.as_str()));
    rest.into_iter().chain(active).collect()
}

/// A whole window destroyed — the red button, or ⌘W on its last tab (that
/// tab was already recorded and removed by `tab_close`). Each caret comes
/// from the last heartbeat when it describes the same tab and file, else the
/// tab starts at the top. Returns how many entries were pushed.
pub fn record_window_close(
    session: &SessionState,
    stack: &ClosedStack,
    label: &str,
    window: &WindowTabs,
) -> usize {
    if session.is_quitting() {
        return 0;
    }
    let snap = session.snapshot_for(label);
    let (x, y, width, height) = snap
        .as_ref()
        .map(|s| (s.x, s.y, s.width, s.height))
        .unwrap_or((0, 0, 0, 0));
    let mut pushed = 0;
    for tab in close_order(window) {
        let Some(path) = tab.path.as_deref() else { continue };
        let (cursor, top_line) = snap
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|s| {
                s.tabs
                    .iter()
                    .find(|t| t.tab_id == tab.id && t.path.as_deref() == Some(path))
            })
            .map(|t| (t.cursor, t.top_line))
            .unwrap_or((0, 1));
        stack.push_entry(ClosedEntry {
            path: path.to_string(),
            cursor,
            top_line,
            label: label.to_string(),
            number: window.number,
            x,
            y,
            width,
            height,
        });
        pushed += 1;
    }
    pushed
}

/// Push `entry` to the top, deduped by path, capped at `MAX_ENTRIES`.
pub fn push(stack: Vec<ClosedEntry>, entry: ClosedEntry) -> Vec<ClosedEntry> {
    let mut out = vec![entry.clone()];
    out.extend(stack.into_iter().filter(|e| e.path != entry.path));
    out.truncate(MAX_ENTRIES);
    out
}

/// Take the most recent entry whose file still exists, dropping every dead
/// entry above it on the way. A file deleted or moved since its tab closed
/// would otherwise reopen as a tab that fails to load, and Cmd+Shift+T would
/// look broken while live entries sit right below it.
pub fn pop_live(
    stack: &mut Vec<ClosedEntry>,
    exists: impl Fn(&str) -> bool,
) -> Option<ClosedEntry> {
    while !stack.is_empty() {
        let entry = stack.remove(0);
        if exists(&entry.path) {
            return Some(entry);
        }
    }
    None
}

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ReopenTarget {
    Window(String),
    NewWindow,
}

/// Where a closed tab comes back: the window it was closed from while it
/// lives; else the window an earlier tab of that same closed window was
/// revived into; else a new window.
pub fn reopen_target(
    entry_label: &str,
    is_live: impl Fn(&str) -> bool,
    revived: &HashMap<String, String>,
) -> ReopenTarget {
    if is_live(entry_label) {
        return ReopenTarget::Window(entry_label.to_string());
    }
    match revived.get(entry_label) {
        Some(new_label) if is_live(new_label) => ReopenTarget::Window(new_label.clone()),
        _ => ReopenTarget::NewWindow,
    }
}

#[derive(Default)]
struct Inner {
    entries: Vec<ClosedEntry>,
    /// Closed window label → the window its tabs are coming back into.
    revived: HashMap<String, String>,
}

/// The live stack, shared by every window in this process. Not persisted —
/// it exists to undo an accidental close a moment ago; session restore covers
/// a restart.
///
/// Lock order: `ClosedStack` → `OpenFiles` (`reopen_closed`'s liveness check
/// runs under this lock). Never lock this while holding `OpenFiles` — read
/// what you need and drop that guard first, as the `Destroyed` handler does.
pub struct ClosedStack(Mutex<Inner>);

impl ClosedStack {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self(Mutex::new(Inner::default()))
    }

    pub fn push_entry(&self, entry: ClosedEntry) {
        let mut inner = self.0.lock().unwrap();
        inner.entries = push(std::mem::take(&mut inner.entries), entry);
    }

    /// Pop the most recently closed entry whose file still exists, if any.
    pub fn pop_live(&self, exists: impl Fn(&str) -> bool) -> Option<ClosedEntry> {
        pop_live(&mut self.0.lock().unwrap().entries, exists)
    }

    pub fn count(&self) -> usize {
        self.0.lock().unwrap().entries.len()
    }

    pub fn target_for(&self, label: &str, is_live: impl Fn(&str) -> bool) -> ReopenTarget {
        reopen_target(label, is_live, &self.0.lock().unwrap().revived)
    }

    pub fn record_revival(&self, closed_label: &str, new_label: &str) {
        self.0
            .lock()
            .unwrap()
            .revived
            .insert(closed_label.to_string(), new_label.to_string());
    }
}

impl Default for ClosedStack {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

/// What `reopen-tab` carries to a live window.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub struct ReopenTab {
    pub path: String,
    pub cursor: usize,
    pub top_line: usize,
}

/// Pop the most recently closed tab and bring it back. Returns `false` (and
/// does nothing) when no live entry is left, so callers can fall back to
/// session restore.
///
/// A file that is open in some tab again by now is not live either:
/// reopening it would only focus that tab and use up the press, while the
/// entry below it — the one the user actually wants back — waits.
pub fn reopen_closed(app: &tauri::AppHandle) -> bool {
    use tauri::{Emitter, Manager};
    let is_open = |p: &str| {
        let open_files = app.state::<crate::window::OpenFiles>();
        let reg = open_files.0.lock().unwrap();
        reg.label_of(p)
            .is_some_and(|label| app.get_webview_window(&label).is_some())
    };
    let stack = app.state::<ClosedStack>();
    let Some(entry) = stack.pop_live(|p| std::path::Path::new(p).exists() && !is_open(p)) else {
        return false;
    };
    let is_live = |label: &str| app.get_webview_window(label).is_some();
    match stack.target_for(&entry.label, is_live) {
        ReopenTarget::Window(label) => {
            if let Some(win) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {
                crate::window::reveal(&win);
                let _ = app.emit_to(
                    label.as_str(),
                    "reopen-tab",
                    ReopenTab {
                        path: entry.path,
                        cursor: entry.cursor,
                        top_line: entry.top_line,
                    },
                );
            }
        }
        ReopenTarget::NewWindow => {
            let closed_label = entry.label.clone();
            let snapshot = crate::session::WindowSnapshot::from_closed_entry(entry);
            if let Some(new_label) = crate::window::open_restored_window(app, &snapshot) {
                stack.record_revival(&closed_label, &new_label);
            }
        }
    }
    true
}

/// Bring the "Reopen…" menu item in line with what Cmd+Shift+T would do now.
/// Call after anything that changes the closed stack or the pending restore.
pub fn refresh_reopen_item(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {
    use tauri::Manager;
    let (Some(items), Some(stack), Some(session)) = (
        app.try_state::<crate::menu::SessionMenuItems>(),
        app.try_state::<ClosedStack>(),
        app.try_state::<SessionState>(),
    ) else {
        return;
    };
    items.sync(stack.count(), session.pending_count());
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::session::TabSnapshot;

    fn entry(path: &str) -> ClosedEntry {
        ClosedEntry {
            path: path.to_string(),
            cursor: 7,
            top_line: 3,
            label: "editor-1".to_string(),
            number: Some(4),
            x: 10,
            y: 20,
            width: 900,
            height: 700,
        }
    }

    fn paths(stack: &[ClosedEntry]) -> Vec<&str> {
        stack.iter().map(|e| e.path.as_str()).collect()
    }

    fn reg_tab(id: &str, path: Option<&str>) -> RegTab {
        RegTab { id: id.to_string(), path: path.map(str::to_string) }
    }

    fn snap_tab(id: &str, path: &str, cursor: usize, top_line: usize) -> TabSnapshot {
        TabSnapshot { tab_id: id.to_string(), path: Some(path.to_string()), untitled: None, cursor, top_line }
    }

    /// `editor-1` at 11,22 800×600, whose last heartbeat reported `tabs`.
    fn session_with(tabs: Vec<TabSnapshot>) -> SessionState {
        let session = SessionState::new();
        session.set_geometry("editor-1", 11, 22, 800, 600);
        session.set_tabs("editor-1", tabs, None);
        session
    }

    #[test]
    fn should_push_on_close_requires_a_path() {
        assert!(should_push_on_close(false, Some("/tmp/a.md")));
        assert!(!should_push_on_close(false, None), "⌘W on untitled is a deliberate discard");
    }

    #[test]
    fn should_push_on_close_is_false_while_quitting() {
        assert!(
            !should_push_on_close(true, Some("/tmp/a.md")),
            "windows closed by a quit come back via session restore"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn push_adds_to_front() {
        let stack = push(vec![entry("/a")], entry("/b"));
        assert_eq!(paths(&stack), vec!["/b", "/a"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn push_dedups_by_path_moving_it_to_front() {
        let stack = push(vec![entry("/a"), entry("/b"), entry("/c")], entry("/b"));
        assert_eq!(paths(&stack), vec!["/b", "/a", "/c"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn push_caps_at_twenty() {
        let mut stack = Vec::new();
        for i in 0..25 {
            stack = push(stack, entry(&format!("/f{}", i)));
        }
        assert_eq!(stack.len(), MAX_ENTRIES);
        assert_eq!(stack[0].path, "/f24", "newest stays on top");
        assert_eq!(stack[MAX_ENTRIES - 1].path, "/f5", "oldest fall off");
    }

    #[test]
    fn stack_pops_most_recent_first() {
        let stack = ClosedStack::new();
        stack.push_entry(entry("/a"));
        stack.push_entry(entry("/b"));
        assert_eq!(stack.pop_live(|_| true).map(|e| e.path), Some("/b".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(stack.pop_live(|_| true).map(|e| e.path), Some("/a".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(stack.count(), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn stack_pop_on_empty_is_none() {
        assert!(ClosedStack::new().pop_live(|_| true).is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn pop_live_skips_and_drops_entries_whose_file_is_gone() {
        let mut stack = vec![entry("/gone1"), entry("/gone2"), entry("/live"), entry("/older")];
        let popped = pop_live(&mut stack, |p| !p.starts_with("/gone"));
        assert_eq!(popped.map(|e| e.path), Some("/live".to_string()));
        assert_eq!(paths(&stack), vec!["/older"], "dead entries above it are discarded");
    }

    #[test]
    fn pop_live_with_only_dead_entries_empties_the_stack() {
        let mut stack = vec![entry("/gone1"), entry("/gone2")];
        assert!(pop_live(&mut stack, |_| false).is_none());
        assert!(stack.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_tab_close_pushes_the_live_caret_with_the_windows_geometry_and_number() {
        let session = session_with(vec![snap_tab("t1", "/tmp/a.md", 1, 1)]);
        let stack = ClosedStack::new();
        assert!(record_tab_close(&session, &stack, "editor-1", Some(7), "/tmp/a.md", 42, 9));
        assert_eq!(
            stack.pop_live(|_| true).unwrap(),
            ClosedEntry {
                path: "/tmp/a.md".to_string(),
                cursor: 42,
                top_line: 9,
                label: "editor-1".to_string(),
                number: Some(7),
                x: 11,
                y: 22,
                width: 800,
                height: 600,
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_tab_close_pushes_nothing_while_quitting() {
        let session = session_with(vec![]);
        session.mark_quitting();
        let stack = ClosedStack::new();
        assert!(!record_tab_close(&session, &stack, "editor-1", None, "/tmp/a.md", 0, 1));
        assert_eq!(stack.count(), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn close_order_puts_the_active_file_tab_last_and_skips_untitled() {
        let window = WindowTabs {
            tabs: vec![reg_tab("a", Some("/a.md")), reg_tab("u", None), reg_tab("b", Some("/b.md"))],
            active: Some("a".to_string()),
            ..WindowTabs::default()
        };
        let ids: Vec<&str> = close_order(&window).iter().map(|t| t.id.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(ids, vec!["b", "a"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_window_close_pushes_every_file_tab_active_on_top() {
        // `b`'s heartbeat still names the file it showed before a Save As.
        let session = session_with(vec![snap_tab("a", "/a.md", 5, 2), snap_tab("b", "/old.md", 9, 9)]);
        let window = WindowTabs {
            tabs: vec![reg_tab("a", Some("/a.md")), reg_tab("u", None), reg_tab("b", Some("/b.md"))],
            active: Some("a".to_string()),
            number: Some(3),
            ..WindowTabs::default()
        };
        let stack = ClosedStack::new();
        assert_eq!(record_window_close(&session, &stack, "editor-1", &window), 2);

        let first = stack.pop_live(|_| true).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((first.path.as_str(), first.cursor, first.top_line), ("/a.md", 5, 2));
        assert_eq!((first.number, first.x, first.width), (Some(3), 11, 800));
        let second = stack.pop_live(|_| true).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            (second.path.as_str(), second.cursor, second.top_line),
            ("/b.md", 0, 1),
            "a caret from another file means nothing here"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn record_window_close_pushes_nothing_while_quitting() {
        let session = session_with(vec![]);
        session.mark_quitting();
        let window = WindowTabs {
            tabs: vec![reg_tab("a", Some("/a.md"))],
            ..WindowTabs::default()
        };
        assert_eq!(record_window_close(&session, &ClosedStack::new(), "editor-1", &window), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn reopen_target_prefers_the_window_it_was_closed_from() {
        assert_eq!(
            reopen_target("editor-1", |_| true, &HashMap::new()),
            ReopenTarget::Window("editor-1".to_string())
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn reopen_target_follows_a_revived_window() {
        let revived = HashMap::from([("editor-1".to_string(), "editor-9".to_string())]);
        assert_eq!(
            reopen_target("editor-1", |l| l == "editor-9", &revived),
            ReopenTarget::Window("editor-9".to_string())
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn reopen_target_opens_a_new_window_when_neither_lives() {
        let revived = HashMap::from([("editor-1".to_string(), "editor-9".to_string())]);
        assert_eq!(reopen_target("editor-1", |_| false, &revived), ReopenTarget::NewWindow);
        assert_eq!(reopen_target("editor-1", |_| false, &HashMap::new()), ReopenTarget::NewWindow);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_recorded_revival_steers_the_next_entry_of_that_window() {
        let stack = ClosedStack::new();
        assert_eq!(stack.target_for("editor-1", |l| l == "editor-9"), ReopenTarget::NewWindow);
        stack.record_revival("editor-1", "editor-9");
        assert_eq!(
            stack.target_for("editor-1", |l| l == "editor-9"),
            ReopenTarget::Window("editor-9".to_string())
        );
    }
}
```

Replace `assign_file_to_main`:

```rust
/// Give `main` a tab for `path` before its frontend mounts, registered in
/// `OpenFiles` (every dedup check consults it) and waiting in its pending
/// payload. A tab, not a replacement: launch arguments may add several.
fn assign_file_to_main(app: &tauri::AppHandle, path: String) {
    let tab_id = session::new_tab_id();
    let added = {
        let open_files = app.state::<OpenFiles>();
        let mut reg = open_files.0.lock().unwrap();
        reg.add_tab("main", &tab_id, Some(path.clone()))
    };
    if !added {
        return;
    }
    let pending = app.state::<PendingFiles>();
    let mut map = pending.0.lock().unwrap();
    let entry = map.entry("main".to_string()).or_default();
    entry.tabs.push(PendingTab {
        tab_id: tab_id.clone(),
        path: Some(path),
        content: None,
        cursor: 0,
        top_line: 1,
    });
    entry.active_tab_id = Some(tab_id);
}
```

### Task 12: Wire the tab controller into App.svelte

App.svelte stops replacing its document and starts switching tabs. `switchDocument`, `switchDocumentNow`, `openInNewWindow`, `loadDocumentInPlace` and the `switchingDocument` flag go; the controller from Task 10 gets its dependencies from the singletons that stay here. Every way a file arrives (⌘O, Recent Files, drag-and-drop, Finder, `open-file`, `reopen-tab`, restore) goes through `tabs.openPath`; AI commands go through `tabs.runExclusive`; the heartbeat reports every tab. The Rust commands only the old path used (`register_open_file`, `release_open_file`, `is_open_elsewhere`) and the one-tab registry helpers are deleted.

```ts
/** A closed tab coming back into this window (⌘⇧T). Matches `ReopenTab` in `closed.rs`. */
export interface ReopenTab {
  path: string;
  cursor: number;
  topLine: number;
}

/** Targeted at the window the tab was closed from — see `onAiCommand` on why per-window. */
export function onReopenTab(handler: (tab: ReopenTab) => void): Promise<() => void> {
  return getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen<ReopenTab>('reopen-tab', (event) => {
    handler(event.payload);
```

```ts
  /** Make `path` the active document for every singleton that follows the active tab. */
  function setActiveDocument(
    path: string | null,
    dirty: boolean,
    baseline: string | null,
    fresh: boolean
  ): void {
    fileState.filePath = path;
    fileState.isDirty = dirty;
    diskBaseline = baseline;
    dismissedDisk = null;
    activePreview = previewKindFor(path);
    // A cached state already carries its language; a fresh one starts as markdown.
    if (fresh) applyFileMode(path);
    // `setCodeMode`/`setEnvMode` reconfigure the preview compartment
    // themselves, with no flavour facet and no live-render bundle; the
    // engine's own configuration goes on top (see `applyPreviewConfig`).
    applyPreviewConfig();
    applyLineGlow();
  }

  function applyFileMode(path: string | null): void {
    const kind = previewKindFor(path);
    const basename = path?.split('/').pop()?.toLowerCase() ?? '';
    const ext = path?.split('.').pop()?.toLowerCase() ?? '';
    if (kind === 'env') {
      editorHandle?.setEnvMode(true);
    } else if (kind === 'markdown') {
      editorHandle?.setEnvMode(false);
      editorHandle?.setCodeMode(null);
    } else {
      editorHandle?.setEnvMode(false);
      editorHandle?.setCodeMode(ext, basename);
    }
  }

  /**
   * Clean the live state for the background, and close what belongs to the
   * window rather than the tab: the search panel, the gutter menu (a module
   * singleton holding the view), the JSON offer's toast, the Recent panel.
   */
  function stripForBackground(): void {
    const view = editorHandle?.view;
    if (view) {
      view.dispatch({ effects: leaveEffects() });
      closeSearchPanel(view);
    }
    hideHoverMenu();
    toasts.dismissKind('json-offer');
    showRecentFiles = false;
  }

  /**
   * The one path that changes what this window shows — see
   * `lib/tabs/controller.ts`. Its dependencies are the singletons that follow
   * the active tab.
   */
  const tabs = createTabController({
    editor: {
      current: () => editorHandle?.view?.state ?? null,
      createState: (doc, cursor) => {
        if (!editorHandle) throw new Error('editor not mounted');
        return editorHandle.createState(doc, cursor);
      },
      swap: (state, fresh) => editorHandle?.swapState(state, { blur: fresh }),
      stripForBackground,
      scrollSnapshot: () => editorHandle?.view?.scrollSnapshot() ?? null,
      restoreScroll: (effect) => editorHandle?.view?.dispatch({ effects: effect }),
      applyPosition: ({ cursor, topLine }) => applyRestorePosition(cursor, topLine),
      topLine: topVisibleLine,
      commitCellEdit: () => activeCellEditSession()?.commit(),
    },
    doc: {
      path: () => fileState.filePath,
      dirty: () => fileState.isDirty,
      baseline: () => diskBaseline,
      setActive: setActiveDocument,
    },
    autosave: {
      flush: () => autoSave.flush(),
      holdsBack: () => conflictDialogOpen,
    },
    saveErrorPending: () => toasts.hasKind('save-error'),
    reportUnsaved: reportSwitchBlockedByUnsaved,
    comments: {
      flush: flushCommentsFor,
      commitPauses: (path) =>
        invoke('commit_document_pauses', { path }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      forget: forgetCommentsFor,
      reload: reloadComments,
    },
    ai: {
      cancel: (path) =>
        invoke('cancel_ai_ask', { path }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      hasLiveAsk: () => {
        const view = editorHandle?.view;
        return view ? activeAskIds(view.state).length > 0 : false;
      },
      clearAsks: () => editorHandle?.view?.dispatch({ effects: clearAiAsks.of(null) }),
    },
    disk: { exists: fileExists, read: readFile },
    rust: {
      owner: (path) =>
        invoke<TabOwner>('tab_owner', { path }).catch((): TabOwner => ({ kind: 'none' })),
      // Without Rust behind the page (`npm run dev` in a browser) a tab still
      // needs an id; it just is not registered anywhere.
      open: (path) =>
        invoke<OpenAnswer>('tab_open', { path }).catch(
          (): OpenAnswer => ({
            kind: 'created',
            tabId: `local-${Date.now()}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}`,
          })
        ),
      release: (tabId) =>
        invoke('tab_release', { tabId }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      activate: (tabId) =>
        invoke('tab_activate', { tabId }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      close: (tabId, { cursor, topLine }) =>
        invoke('tab_close', { tabId, cursor, topLine }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      focusElsewhere: (path) =>
        invoke('focus_if_open', { path }).then(
          () => {},
          () => {}
        ),
      closeWindow: () =>
        import('@tauri-apps/api/window').then(({ getCurrentWindow }) => getCurrentWindow().close()),
    },
    entered: (path, opened) => {
      if (path === null) return;
      if (opened) recentFiles.add(path);
      // A write that landed between the read that loaded this tab and its
      // watcher starting fired no event this window saw; one more read now
      // catches it (and is a no-op when nothing changed).
      void handleExternalChange(path);
    },
    changed: (list) => {
      tabList = list;
    },
    settled: () => reportTabs(),
  });
```

- [ ] **Step 6: AI commands go through the tab queue**

Rename `async function handleAiCommand(payload: AiCommandPayload): Promise<void> {` to `async function handleAiCommandForActive(payload: AiCommandPayload): Promise<void> {`, and delete from its body the `if (payload.firstUse) { … }` block and the `if (switchingDocument) { … }` block together with their comments (the ownership check and everything after it stay). Keep its doc comment (the synchronous-edit invariant). Add above it:

```ts
  const AI_ACTIVATION_ERRORS: Record<'refused' | 'busy' | 'failed', string> = {
    refused: 'the current tab has unsaved changes',
    busy: "the window is showing another agent's question",
    failed: 'could not open the tab',
  };

  /**
   * Route an agent's command to the tab that holds its file.
   *
   * A background tab is activated first, as if the user had clicked it —
   * interim behaviour until plan 04's routing contract (`docs/ai-interface.md`,
   * "Tabs"). It runs in the tab queue, so a command arriving mid-switch waits
   * for the switch instead of failing, and it never takes the view away from
   * another agent's live question.
   */
  async function handleAiCommand(payload: AiCommandPayload): Promise<void> {
    // Before any of the command's own outcomes: an agent has reached this
    // install for the first time, and this is the one moment the user is
    // certain to be looking.
    if (payload.firstUse) {
      toasts.push({ kind: 'ai-first-use' });
    }
    await tabs.runExclusive(async () => {
      if (payload.path !== fileState.filePath) {
        const tab = tabs.findByPath(payload.path);
        if (!tab) {
          await respondToAi(payload.id, { ok: false, error: 'window does not own this file' });
          return;
        }
        const result = await tabs.activateNow(tab.id, { byAgent: true });
        if (result === 'refused' || result === 'busy' || result === 'failed') {
          await respondToAi(payload.id, { ok: false, error: AI_ACTIVATION_ERRORS[result] });
          return;
        }
      }
      await handleAiCommandForActive(payload);
    });
  }
```

```ts
  function reportTabs(): void {
    if (!pendingSettled) return;
    const view = editorHandle?.view;
    if (!view) return;
    const { tabs: reported, active } = tabs.report({
      cursor: view.state.selection.main.head,
      topLine: topVisibleLine(),
      content: view.state.doc.toString(),
    });
    invoke('tabs_sync', { tabs: reported, active }).catch(() => {
      // Session tracking is best-effort; never surface it to the user.
    });
  }
```

### Task 13: The tab keys — ⌘T, ⌘W, ⌘1…⌘9, ⌃Tab, ⌃⇧Tab, ⌘⇧T

Spec §6 «Клавиатура»: every key goes into `menu.rs` and into the `native-menu-accelerators.ts` mirror (its test parses `menu.rs` and fails on any divergence). ⌘W stops being Rust's "close the focused window" and becomes the frontend's "close the active tab" (the last tab closes the window). ⌘⇧T keeps its menu id; after Task 11 it reopens tabs.

Conflicts checked: `CmdOrCtrl+T`, `CmdOrCtrl+1…9` and `Ctrl+Tab` are unused by the menu (`CmdOrCtrl+0` is Reset Zoom, `CmdOrCtrl+Shift+T` is Reopen) and unbound in CodeMirror's `defaultKeymap`/`historyKeymap`/`searchKeymap`/`foldKeymap`, `markdownKeybindings` and the slash/autocomplete keymaps — `Tab` there is bound without modifiers, which never matches `Ctrl-Tab`. Native Cmd accelerators win over the webview (the reason `format_json`'s menu path is the one that runs); `Ctrl+Tab` is the one to verify by hand (Step 7).

- Modify: `src/lib/editor/native-menu-accelerators.ts`
- Modify: `src-tauri/src/menu.rs` (File: New Tab; View → Tabs submenu)
- Modify: `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` (delete the Rust `close` branch)
- Modify: `locales/{en,ru,de,es,fr,zh}/native.json`

- [ ] **Step 6: The keys in `dev:app`, with real key presses**

Synthetic webview events cannot reach native menu accelerators, so press the keys for real. Launch `npm run dev:app`; from another terminal send keystrokes with System Events (needs Accessibility permission for the terminal; if macOS refuses, ask the owner to press them and note the result):

```sh
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke "t" using command down'
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to key code 48 using control down'
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to key code 48 using {control down, shift down}'
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke "1" using command down'
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke "w" using command down'
osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke "t" using {command down, shift down}'
```

**Expected:** ⌘T opens an untitled tab after the active one; ⌘1 selects the first tab; ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab move to the next / previous tab and wrap; ⌘W on a saved file closes its tab and activates the right neighbour; ⌘⇧T brings it back into the same window at its caret; ⌘W on the last tab closes the window. Check the File and View → Tabs menus show each key.

- [ ] **Step 7: Only if ⌃Tab did not switch tabs in Step 6**

WKWebView may consume Control-Tab for its own focus navigation before the menu sees the key equivalent. If — and only if — Step 6 showed the menu item's ⌃Tab doing nothing while clicking **View → Tabs → Show Next Tab** works, add a capture-phase fallback in `App.svelte`'s `onMount` (keep the menu items: they document the keys):

```ts
    // WKWebView keeps Control-Tab for its own focus navigation, so the menu's
    // key equivalent never fires; matched on `e.code`, layout-independent.
    function handleControlTab(e: KeyboardEvent): void {
      if (!e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey || e.altKey || e.code !== 'Tab') return;
      e.preventDefault();
      e.stopPropagation();
      void tabs.cycle(e.shiftKey ? -1 : 1);
    }
    window.addEventListener('keydown', handleControlTab, { capture: true });
```

with `window.removeEventListener('keydown', handleControlTab, { capture: true });` in the cleanup. Re-run Step 6's ⌃Tab lines and confirm exactly **one** step per press (two steps would mean both paths fire — then remove the fallback again, the menu works after all). Record the outcome in the commit message.

### Task 14: A temporary tab list (throwaway until plan 03)

Enough UI to see a window's tabs and to click them — for this plan's verification and for daily use until plan 03's drawer (`docs/investigations/2026-09-24-tabs-mockup/drawer.html`) replaces it. No design, no drawer behaviour, no keyboard model, no a11y roles; plan 03 deletes the file. Shown only when a window holds more than one tab, so a one-tab window looks exactly as before.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/lib/TabListTemp.svelte`
- Modify: `src/App.svelte` (render it)

- [ ] **Step 1: The component**

`src/lib/TabListTemp.svelte`:

```svelte
<!--
  TEMPORARY — tabs plan 02 only. A bare list to see and drive a window's
  tabs until plan 03's drawer replaces it wholesale
  (docs/investigations/2026-09-24-tabs-mockup/drawer.html). No design, no
  keyboard model, no a11y roles on purpose: do not build on it.
-->
<script lang="ts">
  import type { TabListState } from './tabs/tab-model';
  import { t } from './i18n';

  let {
    list,
    activeDirty,
    onactivate,
    onclose,
  }: {
    list: TabListState;
    /** The active tab's dirty flag lives in `fileState`, not in the list. */
    activeDirty: boolean;
    onactivate: (tabId: string) => void;
    onclose: (tabId: string) => void;
  } = $props();

  function name(path: string | null): string {
    return path ? (path.split('/').pop() ?? path) : t('ui.untitled');
  }
</script>

{#if list.tabs.length > 1}
  <nav class="tab-list-temp" data-testid="tab-list-temp">
    {#each list.tabs as tab, i (tab.id)}
      {@const active = tab.id === list.activeId}
      <div class="tab" class:active data-tab-id={tab.id}>
        <button class="name" title={tab.path ?? ''} onclick={() => onactivate(tab.id)}>
          {#if i < 9}<span class="key">⌘{i + 1}</span>{/if}{(active ? activeDirty : tab.dirty)
            ? '● '
            : ''}{name(tab.path)}
        </button>
        <button class="close" data-testid="tab-close" onclick={() => onclose(tab.id)}>×</button>
      </div>
    {/each}
  </nav>
{/if}

<style>
  .tab-list-temp {
    position: fixed;
    left: 8px;
    bottom: 8px;
    z-index: 870;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 2px;
    max-width: 40vw;
    max-height: 50vh;
    overflow-y: auto;
    padding: 4px 6px;
    font: 11px/1.5 var(--font-text);
    color: var(--text-muted);
    background: var(--bg-surface);
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-radius: 6px;
  }

  .tab {
    display: flex;
    align-items: center;
    gap: 4px;
  }

  .tab.active .name {
    color: var(--text-primary);
    font-weight: 600;
  }

  button {
    all: unset;
    cursor: pointer;
  }

  .name {
    flex: 1;
    overflow: hidden;
    text-overflow: ellipsis;
    white-space: nowrap;
  }

  .key {
    margin-right: 6px;
    opacity: 0.6;
  }

  .close {
    padding: 0 4px;
    opacity: 0.5;
  }

  .close:hover {
    opacity: 1;
  }
</style>
```

In **Architecture**, add to the Rust list (after `window.rs`):

```
  menu_route.rs         # Which window a menu action reaches: preferences to all, document actions to the last-focused one
  tabs.rs               # Tab registry behind OpenFiles: window → #N, ordered tabs, active; one file = one tab app-wide
  tab_commands.rs       # Tab IPC (tab_owner/open/claim/release/activate/close), decided under the registry lock
  window_numbers.rs     # #N allocator (1–99, wrap, skip live) + persisted counter
```

and to the frontend list (after `lib/ai-commands.ts`):

```
  lib/tabs/             # Tabs, B-lite: only the active tab is a live state
    tab-model.ts        # Pure tab list operations (insert after active, close → neighbour, ⌘N, ⌃Tab)
    tab-cache.ts        # What is stripped from a state going to the background; reuse-or-reload on return
    controller.ts       # The one path that changes what a window shows (open/activate/new/close/init; AI via runExclusive)
  lib/editor/state-factory.ts # The one EditorState factory — a fresh state per load, so no undo leaks across documents
  lib/comment-writer.ts # Serialized comment sidecar writes (draft → real id)
  lib/window-title.ts   # "name — #N"
  lib/TabListTemp.svelte # TEMPORARY tab list — plan 03's drawer replaces it
```

In **Key Files**, add:

```
- `src/lib/tabs/controller.ts` — **Read before changing how a window opens, switches or closes a document.** The check → prepare → hand over → swap → settle order, and why nothing may `await` between the last check and the swap
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-tabs-02-model.md` — Tab model plan: design decisions D1–D17
```

In **Gotchas**, add:

```
- **A window's `emit` is a broadcast, and a global `listen` also receives targeted emits.** Emitting per window in a loop while the frontend listens globally delivers every event N× to each of N windows — how every menu action ran N×N times until tabs plan 02. Emit once (`app.emit` for everyone, `emit_to(label)` for one window) and listen per window (`getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen`). Menu routing lives in `menu_route.rs`; the target is the last `WindowEvent::Focused(true)`, because `is_focused()` is unreliable while the menu bar is in use.
- **`EditorView.setState` runs no update listeners, and compartments belong to the state.** A swapped-in state (a cached tab, a fresh load) keeps whatever preview/line-glow/language configuration it was built or left with, and nothing derived from `ViewUpdate` (AI-highlight presence → the hint badge) notices the swap. `swapState` recomputes the highlight presence; `setActiveDocument` reapplies the engine and line glow after every swap. A language that finishes loading after its tab was left is dropped (`modeGeneration` in `Editor.svelte`).
- **Tab ownership changes only through `tab_commands.rs`.** `tabs_sync` (the heartbeat) reorders and reports but never claims a path, and never drops a tab it did not hear about — a claim can be in flight while an older report is on its way.
- **Background file tabs are always clean, and unwatched.** Leaving a file tab whose save has not landed is refused (a cached state has no autosave, recovery snapshot or watcher). Returning to a tab compares the disk with the text it held when it was left: equal → the cached state (undo intact), different → a fresh load at the old caret.
- **Session v2 lives in `session-v2.json`; `session.json` is read once and never written** (migration v1 → v2, one tab per window). A corrupt v2 falls back to v1. Untitled sidecars are named by tab id.
```

Change "Use `npx vitest run --dir src` for a true count (513 at the time of writing)" to "(1414 after tabs plan 02)".

- [ ] **Step 2: `docs/ai-interface.md`**

Add a section before `## Troubleshooting`:

```markdown
## Tabs (interim, until the routing contract)

Windows hold tabs, and one file is open in at most one tab in the whole app. Until tabs plan 04 lands the routing contract (`window_binding`, `focus`, `transient`), a command still goes to the window that holds its file, and:

| Situation | What happens |
|---|---|
| The file is that window's active tab | As before. |
| The file is a background tab of that window | The window activates that tab, as if it had been clicked, then handles the command. |
| … and the active tab has unsaved changes | `{"ok":false,"error":"the current tab has unsaved changes"}` |
| … and the active tab shows another agent's live `ask` | `{"ok":false,"error":"the window is showing another agent's question"}` — an agent never takes the view away from a pending question |
| The file is not open anywhere | As before: a new window opens with it. |
| The tab holding a pending request is left | `switched away from this document` — `ask` included: a question does not survive in a background tab yet |
| The tab holding a pending or queued request is closed | `tab closed` |

Commands arriving while a window is switching tabs wait for the switch instead of failing.
```

In the Troubleshooting table: delete the `window is switching documents` row (no longer produced); change the `switched away from this document` row's cause to "The user switched that window to another tab (or replaced its document) before the request was answered — for `ask`, before an option was clicked."; and add rows:

```markdown
| `{"ok":false,"error":"tab closed"}` (`show`, `edit`, `ask`) | The tab holding the file was closed before the request was answered (or before a queued request was delivered). | Re-send; `mdmini show <file>` reopens it. |
| `{"ok":false,"error":"the current tab has unsaved changes"}` | The file is in a background tab and the window could not leave its active tab: that tab's latest save has not landed. | Retry in a moment. |
| `{"ok":false,"error":"the window is showing another agent's question"}` | The file is in a background tab, and the active tab shows a pending `ask`. | Wait for that question to be answered, or ask the user to switch tabs. |
| `{"ok":false,"error":"could not open the tab"}` | The background tab's file could no longer be read (permissions, not valid text). | Check the file. |
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Manual scenarios in `dev:app`**

`lsof -ti:1430 | xargs kill -9 2>/dev/null; npm run dev:app -- --features mcp-bridge --config '{"app":{"windows":[{"title":"tabs-02 · local"}]}}'`. Real key presses via the `osascript` lines from Task 13 Step 6 (or by the owner). Use scratch files under `/tmp/tabs02/` (`mkdir -p /tmp/tabs02 && for f in a b c; do printf "# $f\n\nline\n" > /tmp/tabs02/$f.md; done`).

1. **One delivery per window.** Two windows; ⌘E once → each window's engine moves one step.
2. **Keys.** ⌘T, ⌘1…⌘3, ⌃Tab/⌃⇧Tab wrap, ⌘W → right neighbour, last ⌘W closes the window.
3. **Undo per tab.** Type in `a.md`, ⌘O `b.md`, ⌘1: ⌘Z undoes the typing in `a.md`; ⌘2, ⌘Z does nothing from `a.md`.
4. **⌘W flushes.** Type quickly in `a.md` and press ⌘W within a quarter second; ⌘⇧T: the last keystrokes are there and the caret is where it was.
5. **Untitled tabs persist.** ⌘T type `one`, ⌘T type `two`, ⌘Q, relaunch, Restore → both come back with their text; `ls ~/Library/Application\ Support/md-mini-dev/session/` shows two `untitled-<id>.md`.
6. **Untitled ⌘W discards.** ⌘T type text, ⌘W: gone, and ⌘⇧T does not bring it back.
7. **Dedup across windows.** `a.md` as tab 2 of window #1; in window #2 ⌘O `a.md` → window #1 comes forward with `a.md` active; no second copy.
8. **External change of a background tab.** `a.md` in the background; `echo change >> /tmp/tabs02/a.md`; ⌘1 → the new line is there, no dialog.
9. **AI into a background tab.** `a.md` in the background: `mdmini show /tmp/tabs02/a.md --line 3` → the window switches to `a.md` and pulses line 3. With `mdmini ai ask /tmp/tabs02/b.md --question Q --option x --option y` pending in the active `b.md`, `mdmini show /tmp/tabs02/a.md` → `the window is showing another agent's question`, `b.md` stays.
10. **Ask on leave / on close.** With that ask pending in `b.md`, ⌘1 → the ask call returns `switched away from this document` at once. Repeat, then ⌘W on `b.md` → `tab closed`.
11. **Red-button close and ⌘⇧T.** A window #N with `a`, `b`, `c` (`b` active): red button, then ⌘⇧T three times → one new window `#N`, `b` first, then `c`, `a`, each at its caret.
12. **Titles and numbers.** `a.md — #N · md-mini-dev`; a new window takes the next number; after quit + relaunch + Restore windows keep their numbers and a fresh window's number is above them.
13. **v1 migration.** Quit; move `session-v2.json` aside; relaunch with the v1 `session.json` left from before this plan → Restore offers and restores its windows (one tab each); `session.json`'s mtime is unchanged.
14. **Save error blocks leaving.** `chmod 444 /tmp/tabs02/c.md`, open it, type → the save-error toast; ⌘1 and ⌘W do nothing and the toast stays; `chmod 644`, type → saved, toast gone, ⌘1 works.
15. **Save As claims.** ⌘T, type, ⌘⇧S to `/tmp/tabs02/d.md`; in another window ⌘O `d.md` → this tab comes forward.
16. **Comment race.** Select a word, ⌘⇧M, type a sentence and press ⌘2 immediately → no comment-error toast; ⌘1 → the thread exists with the full sentence.
17. **Temp list.** With two or more tabs the list shows bottom-left; clicking names switches, × closes a background tab without switching.

For anything that fails: fix it, re-run the affected automated suite, and commit the fix separately with an accurate message.

| Spec | Where |

| §3 numbers 1–99, ascending, wrap skipping live, counter in app data, stable across restarts, `README.md — #7` | Task 6 (+ Task 11: a reopened window keeps its number when free). Dead-number error and CLI `-t` are plan 04. |
| §6 ⌘T, ⌘W (last tab closes the window), ⌘1…⌘9, ⌃Tab/⌃⇧Tab wrapping, ⌘⇧T (fresh launch → restore session: unchanged `reopen_session` fallback) | Tasks 10, 13. ⌘J, letters, ⌘L/⌘R/⌘U are plan 03. |
| §6 all new keys in `menu.rs` and the mirror | Task 13 |
| §8 ⌘W on untitled discards; saved files close without loss; closed stack in Rust with position; undo of a closed tab not restored | Tasks 10 (flush + discard rule), 11 (stack of tabs), D16 |
| §10 B-lite: only the active tab live, singletons stay singletons; background `{path, pos, EditorState}`; mtime + hash on return | Tasks 9, 10, 12; content compare instead of mtime (D5) |
| §10 `WindowTabs` with `OpenFiles` for dedup; `AiPending`/`AiQueue` by (label, path) | Tasks 4, 7 |
| §10 session v2 in `session-v2.json`, v1 untouched, migration, window number kept | Tasks 5, 6. The window's *project* is plan 04 (it adds a `project` field with a serde default). |
| §10 Cmd+W to the frontend | Tasks 1 (reliable routing), 12, 13 |
| Controller mandates 1–6 | Tasks 1, 13 (menu → one window, ⌘W); 10 (orchestration + tests); 7, 10, 12 (AI keyed by (label, path), background-tab behaviour D7); 3 (comment race), 10 (typing during handover), 12 (read-before-watch post-check); 2 (`unsaved-blocked`) |
| Temporary tab list | Task 14 |

## Known gaps and risks carried out of this plan

- **Red button and ⌘Q still skip the last ≤300 ms debounce** of the active tab (D17). Background tabs are clean, so exposure equals today's.
- **⌃Tab may be swallowed by WKWebView** before the menu's key equivalent; Task 13 Step 7 has the measured fallback.
- **Finder / `mdmini a.md` still open a new window per file** when `main` already shows a file — routing a file into an existing window's tab is plan 04 (§4, §5).
- **An `ask` does not survive its tab going to the background**; it is cancelled with an error (D6) until plan 04 re-delivers it.
- **Returning to a tab whose file changed on disk loses that tab's undo history** — accepted in the research (B-lite trade-off).
- Session v2 must not ship in the same release as the rebrand's data-directory migration (roadmap).
